the code and tests in this tree. Original sweep: debt-sweep worktree, HEAD
`14bc2cb6`, dated 2026-09-12. **Gap-closure pass** (historical sweep): same
worktree, starting HEAD `c287f5fb`, also dated 2026-09-12 — an independent
verifier found coverage gaps in the first pass (missing items across all
five design-doc ledgers, stale line-number citations, and one row,

completion pass** (historical sweep): same worktree, starting HEAD
`56992663`, also dated 2026-09-12 — a second independent verifier found
the tree otherwise accurate and two remaining gaps: one IMPROVEMENTS.md
heading (the GGX low-F0 grazing gain) not yet carried into the table as

and tests the way `## Already resolved` requires (now DL-R18..DL-R23, one
of which — DL-R20 — turned out to be resolved-in-code but only indirectly
tested, noted as such rather than silently closed); see `## Counts` for
the delta. **Re-sweep** (this pass): debt-resweep worktree, HEAD
`876c9a26`, dated 2026-09-12 — run after a separate cleanup session closed
16 rows (DL-01/02/03/04/34/36/37/48/50/51/54/55/56/57/58/59) and added
DL-38..DL-62 without a further independent check. This pass re-verified
all 47 rows left OPEN against `876c9a26`'s actual source, cross-checked
against the cleanup session's touched-file list, and found the ledger's
OPEN/CLOSED partition already correct (0 rows reclassified); one stale
citation on DL-19 was corrected in place (see `## Counts`). Every verdict

Sorted by class (physics-bias/energy-loss, precision, API/bridge gap,
coverage/test gap, perf, doc-rot), S before M before L within a class (the

| DL-67 | Found during DL-42/DL-43 (debt-guiding slice, 2026-09-13; filed in this slice's own ledger as DL-65, RENUMBERED DL-67 at merge time -- see `## Counts` -- to avoid colliding with rows concurrently filed by sibling debt-cleanup slices: DL-65 is the ggx slice's row, DL-66 the sssenv slice's, DL-68 the translucent slice's) | PT's and BDPT's guided one-sample and RIS proposal/throughput densities are inconsistent across lobe-selection branches at a multi-lobe SPF: neither RIS candidate 0 nor the one-sample/RIS "aggregate BSDF" branches use the TRUE generating density of the direction they price | OPEN-confirmed (static + closed-form counterexample; rendered red-proof pending) | Root cause: at a multi-lobe SPF, candidate 0's (and any kray-based branch's) TRUE generating density for a direction is the mixture `sum_I q_I * p_I` over every lobe `Scatter()` could have emitted, where `q_I = PTScatterSelectWeight(lobe_I) / sum_j PTScatterSelectWeight(lobe_j)` (`PathTracingIntegrator.cpp:1337`, `MaxValue(kray)`-weighted) -- NOT the selected lobe's own `pS->pdf`, and NOT the material's aggregate `ISPF::Pdf()` either, because `ISPF::Pdf()` may re-weight lobes by a different ratio than `PTRandomlySelect` used to pick one. (The original example here was `SchlickSPF::Pdf`, which weighted diffuse/specular by `MaxValue(rd)` vs `MaxValue(rs)` -- the raw painter albedos -- while `PTScatterSelectWeight`/`PTRandomlySelect` weight the SAME two lobes by `MaxValue(diffuse kray)` vs `MaxValue(rho+(1-rho)*fresnel)`. **That example is HISTORICAL as of 2026-09-14**: Slice 0 replaced it with the true generating density (see the Slice 0 note at the end of this cell), so `SchlickSPF::Pdf` is no longer an instance. `IsotropicPhongSPF::Pdf` -- symbol, not line number -- still is: DL-98.). So: (1) candidate 0 (`c.bsdfPdf = pS->pdf`, `PathTracingIntegrator.cpp:3137`; `BDPTIntegrator.cpp:2537` eye / `:6276` light) uses the wrong (single-lobe) density, and candidate 1's aggregate `PTEvalPdfAtSurface`/`PathValueOps::EvalPdfAtVertex` uses a DIFFERENT wrong (aggregate-but-mismatched-weights) density -- both miss the true `sum_I q_I p_I`, so RIS's `sum_i P_i/D_i = M` identity is violated: a closed-form 2-candidate example with `p1=10, p2=0.1` (per-lobe pdfs), guide pdf `p_g=1`, true aggregate `p_agg=5.05` gives per-branch coefficients 1.074 (candidate-0-style) vs 0.256 (aggregate-style) against the correct value, i.e. roughly a 33% low estimate using the true generating density as ground truth. (2) The same inconsistency exists between PT's THREE one-sample/RIS overwrite branches in `PathTracingIntegrator.cpp`'s trained-guiding block: branch (a), this slice's DL-42 fix (`PTScatterKray(*pS) * pS->pdf/(selectProb*combinedPdf)`, per-lobe kray/pdf); branch (c), the one-sample guided-direction-accepted branch (`PTMulDiv(fGuided,cosTheta,combinedPdf)`, aggregate f over aggregate-but-mismatched pdf); and the RIS-accepted branch (b). (a) and (c) do not partition to a consistent total unless every lobe shares one pdf (single-lobe SPF) -- adding `/q_I` to (c) does not fix this; the correct construction uses one `f` and one denominator (the true mixture density `sum_I q_I p_I` over the lobes `Scatter()` actually accepted) in ALL THREE branches, in both PT and BDPT eye/light. Scope: any multi-emit SPF (SchlickSPF, PolishedSPF/PolishedBRDF, TranslucentSPF, CompositeSPF, BioSpecSkinSPF, GenericHumanTissueSPF); `GGXSPF`/`CoatedSPF` are immune (internal single-lobe selection: exactly one `ScatteredRay` per `Scatter()` call, `pdf==mixPdf`, outer `selectProb` trivially 1). A separate, additive asymmetry in the same candidate machinery: candidate 1 is discarded (`c.valid=false`, `risWeight=0`) whenever the material's aggregate pdf is 0 at the guided direction (`PathTracingIntegrator.cpp:3148` candidate 0 / `:3177` candidate 1 / `:3191` fallback; `BDPTIntegrator.cpp:2547`/`:2584`/`:2598` eye, `:6286`/`:6314`/`:6328` light), silently falling back to candidate 0 alone and losing whatever variance-reduction the guide would have offered there -- energy-losing in the sense of discarding a legitimate RIS candidate, tracked as part of the same rework rather than a fourth bug. Recipe: red-prove on `schlick_material` (diffuse+specular, both eligible, overlapping support) with guiding forced to substitute (`guidingAlpha` near 1, a real trained field so `guidedSubstituted>0`, unlike this slice's DL-42 fixture which forced `guidedSubstituted=0`), comparing rendered radiance against unguided PT reference; fix = the one-f/one-denominator construction above in PT (all three branches) and BDPT eye/light RIS. **Slice 0 landed 2026-09-14** (`docs/DL67_SLICE0_SCHLICK_PDF_WEIGHTS.md`, branch `debt-slice0`): `SchlickSPF::Pdf`/`PdfNM` now returns the ACTUAL density of the direction `Scatter` + `RandomlySelect` produce -- the `ISPF::Pdf()` half of this row's own root-cause paragraph. The diffuse lobe's coefficient `C_D = E_{(u,v)}[P(the diffuse ray wins | that specular draw)]` is computed by a DETERMINISTIC 16x16 stratified quadrature that REPLAYS `GenerateSpecularRay` in its own `(xi,b)` inverse-CDF parametrisation (sharing `SchlickSampleHalfVector` with the sampler, and reproducing Scatter's accept-checks and `RandomlySelect`'s `freeidx==1`/underflow branches); the specular coefficient is exact at the query direction; the per-channel branch's three lanes (which share ONE random pair) are handled exactly FOR THE INTENDED SEMANTICS by inverting the sampler at the query direction and replaying the other two lanes (the sampler's own per-channel branch deviates from that intended semantics while **DL-101** is open, per the round-2 review -- see the doc's SS2 and SS7); and a tilted shading normal is modelled by the exact clipped-cosine-hemisphere fraction `A_D=(1+dot(n,geomN))/2`. **This CORRECTS, not extends, the first version of this slice**, which used `MaxValue(rd)/(MaxValue(rd)+SchlickFresnelAvg(rs))` for `C_D`: that is the wrong quantity three times over (measured, see the doc's §3 table): the hemispherical average is under the wrong measure (mild -- 0.9009 vs 0.9048 at `rs=0.9`, but 0.1961 vs 0.0667 at `rs=0.02`/80 degrees); `C_D = E[w_D/(w_D+w_S)]` is a CONVEX function of `w_S`, so substituting any average of `w_S` is a different number by Jensen regardless; and the term that actually dominates is not a reflectance at all but the specular sampler's REJECTION rate -- `Scatter` accepts only 0.739/0.640/0.577 of its specular draws at 30/60/80 degrees incidence, and a rejected specular draw leaves `RandomlySelect` holding one ray, returned with probability 1. Even a rejection-aware closed form (`A*rd/(rd+rs)+(1-A)`) still misses true `C_D` by up to 13.7%, so there was no closed form to reach for. Measured, the first version's aggregate integrated to 0.68-1.02 instead of 1 and its total variation against the real sampler was WORSE than the code it replaced on 6 of the 8 review configurations (by up to 2.4x), a wash on a seventh and better on the eighth. Its two gates could not see this: one compared `Pdf()` against a replica of its own formula, the other asserted `Pdf(w_S) >= q_S p_S`, an identity for any non-negative `C_D`. Both were deleted. Red-proof is now a two-sided normalisation gate (a hemisphere quadrature of `Pdf` against the MEASURED probability that `Scatter` emits any ray, over 600k real calls) plus a total-variation gate against a 600k-draw histogram of real `Scatter`+`RandomlySelect` draws, over 12 configurations, RGB and NM: `Checks: 34 Failures: 34` at `4325f365`, `33` at pre-slice `32824325`, **`0` after**, with every `|int Pdf - emitted|` <= 0.0062 and every TVD <= 0.0081 against a derived MC-noise floor of 0.0051. Two further defects of the same pattern were found while measuring and fixed in the same commit: the anisotropic AZIMUTHAL DENSITY was a different distribution from the one the sampler draws (up to 25x off at isotropy 0.3, measured against a 2e6-draw histogram; recorded as **DL-102**, closed), and `ComputeSchlickSpecularPdf` built its half-vector in a FlipW'd frame on the false premise that `GenerateSpecularRay` does (that function's `onb` parameter was dead; it always used `ri.onb`). `SPFPdfConsistencyTest`'s Schlick row consequently tightened from a 15% integral tolerance to the standard 5% (measured 1.00166/0.999932) and its chi2 is now GATED (783.7/816.3 vs critical 928.3) instead of skipped; its old note blaming "the specular PDF normalisation assumes isotropic roughness" was a misdiagnosis and is corrected in place. Cost: `Pdf()` 17.0 ns -> 690 ns (2003 ns on the 3-lane per-channel branch); whole-render CPU +2.5% (inside a +/-4% spread) on a Cornell box where 1 of 15 objects is schlick, +26% on a variant where every surface is. Rendered frame-mean luminance on that scene moved 0.995781 +/- 0.000318 -> 0.997451 +/- 0.000065 over 4 runs each (+0.168%). This does NOT close the row: candidate 0/1's own PT/BDPT branch-level fix (this row's actual subject) is unstarted. Sibling audit opened DL-98, DL-99, DL-100, DL-101 and closed DL-102; `CookTorranceSPF`/`GGXSPF`/`CoatedSPF`/`PolishedSPF`/Ward/`SchlickBRDF` confirmed immune or already correct on THIS row's pattern. **2026-09-14 (debt-dl69 slice, DL-69 closed):** the shared root cause's ORDINARY (non-guiding) half is now fixed -- BDPT/VCM/MLT's unconditional non-delta throughput pairs the selected lobe's own `kray_I` with its own `selectProb`, and `pdfFwd` is the aggregate `ISPF::Pdf()` that `pdfRev` already used. That slice deliberately did NOT touch either guided branch: when guiding SUBSTITUTED a direction, `BDPTIntegrator.cpp`'s eye and light generators still price the aggregate `EvalBSDFAtVertex` over `selectProb * guidedEffectivePdf`. **`pdfFwd` there is NOT "still `selectProb * effectivePdf`", as this cell claimed until the 2026-09-14 review**: the new `pdfFwdPrev` block carries no `usedGuidedDirection` guard, so under substitution `pdfFwd` is the material's AGGREGATE `ISPF::Pdf()` evaluated at the GUIDED direction (`scatDir`, which the substitution overwrote with `guidedDir`), with `selectProb * effectivePdf` surviving only as the `misFwdPdf <= NEARZERO` fallback. That is a THIRD density -- neither the guided sampling density nor a mixture that includes the guide -- so DL-67's own reconciliation has to account for it rather than assume the branch was left alone -- so DL-67's scope is unchanged except that its §2.5 "define candidate 0's `bsdfPdf` via `PTEvalPdfAtSurface`" step now has a same-file precedent to match (the `pdfFwdPrev` block in both generators). What DID change under DL-67's feet: the guiding-BLENDED-density branch (`bsdfCombinedPdf > NEARZERO`, the kept-BSDF-direction case) now takes PT's own DL-42-fixed per-lobe shape `kray_I * pScat->pdf / (selectProb * combinedPdf)` instead of the aggregate form, so BDPT and PT agree there and DL-67 no longer needs to reconcile them. | M-L | physics-bias | user-visible (guided renders of any multi-lobe, multi-emit material) |
| ~~DL-69~~ | ~~Found during DL-42/DL-67 derivation (debt-guiding slice, 2026-09-13)~~ | ~~BDPT's ORDINARY (non-guiding, unconditional) eye/light non-delta throughput pairs the material's AGGREGATE (all-lobes-summed) BSDF value with a PER-LOBE selection pdf, over-counting by up to Nx at a multi-lobe SPF whose lobes have overlapping support~~ Both generators now pair the SELECTED lobe's own `kray_I` with that lobe's own selection probability -- the delta branch's and PT's shipped formula -- and store the material's AGGREGATE, direction-only `ISPF::Pdf()` as `pdfFwd`, the same function `pdfRev` and every connection strategy already evaluate | CLOSED 2026-09-14 (debt-dl69 slice) -- `19d97505`; `BDPTStrategyBalanceTest: Passed 72, Failed 0` and `VCMStrategyBalanceTest: Passed 61, Failed 0` (both including a new `schlick_material` topology L) at the time of the fix; on the merge target, with the review's added topology M and DL-125 probe, `BDPTStrategyBalanceTest: Passed 82, Failed 0`, `VCMStrategyBalanceTest: Passed 61, Failed 0`, `SchlickLobePairingTest: Passed 24, Failed 0` | Full record: docs/DL69_BDPT_LOBE_THROUGHPUT.md. **Red-proof (unfixed library, `721e8f12`)**: topology L (schlick_material wall + floor, large area emitter, depth-matched PT reference, 32x32 at 256 spp) measured BDPT mean +19.3686% / p99 +63.0327% / max +981.135% over PT (`BDPTStrategyBalanceTest: Passed 69, Failed 3`) and VCM mean +32.7409% / p99 +76.8255% / max +469.335% (`VCMStrategyBalanceTest: Passed 58, Failed 3`); post-fix BDPT -2.00% and VCM -2.12% on the mean, all three metrics inside the suite bands. Closed-form: `tests/SchlickLobePairingTest.cpp` measures all three arithmetically-available pairings for one selected lobe of a two-lobe overlapping-support `SchlickSPF` against a BRDF quadrature reference Q at 0/30/60 deg -- per-lobe `kray_I/q_I` = 1.000/0.961/0.802 Q, aggregate-over-aggregate `f_agg cos/ISPF::Pdf` = 1.011/0.997/0.999 Q, and the mismatched pairing BDPT used = **1.999/2.000/2.004 Q**, the exact N-times over-count for N=2; the same test measures `(q_I p_I)/ISPF::Pdf()` spanning [0.0035, 1.281] across real draws, red-proving the second half (pdfFwd and pdfRev were two different formulas for one MIS density). **Those closed-form figures were RE-MEASURED 2026-09-14 against the merge target** (master `e290fc64`, which brings DL-67 Slice 0's `SchlickSPF::Pdf` rewrite); the pre-merge readings were 0.999/0.961/0.802, 0.999/1.000/1.001, 1.998/1.999/2.006 and a spread of [0.003, 1.724]. The aggregate estimator itself had to be repaired to survive that merge: it drew its lobe with a FIXED 50/50 coin over the realized container, which is unbiased only while `ISPF::Pdf` IS that fixed mixture, and against Slice 0's true generating density it read 0.842/0.821/0.793 Q (`Passed: 21 Failed: 3`); it now draws with the REAL `RandomlySelect` rule, the procedure `ISPF::Pdf` is the density of. **Per-(s,t) strategy balance** (temporary in-tree instrumentation, since removed; same scene): every s=0 row -- the BSDF-sampled emitter-hit strategy, the one the deflated `pdfFwd` suppressed -- gains MIS weight (t=3 0.3161->0.4144 +31.1%, t=4 0.2262->0.3325 +47.0%, t=5 0.2636->0.3477 +31.9%, t=6 0.1762->0.3165 +79.7%), while row (1,2) -- whose eye endpoint is the FIRST surface vertex, so no multi-lobe scatter enters it -- is unchanged to four decimals as an internal control. **Sibling audit refutes two claims in this row's own original text**: `PolishedSPF` is NOT disjoint -- its dielectric coat lobe is non-delta whenever `scattering` is finite (Phong `< 1e6` / HG `g < 1`) and the diffuse substrate is added whenever `MinValue(Rs) < 1`, both in the same hemisphere, so a glossy `polished_material` over a visible substrate carried the defect; and the row under-enumerated the affected set -- `WardIsotropicGaussianSPF`, `WardAnisotropicEllipticalGaussianSPF`, `IsotropicPhongSPF`, `AshikminShirleyAnisotropicPhongSPF` and `CompositeSPF` all emit overlapping non-delta lobes with per-lobe `.pdf` and were affected too. Confirmed IMMUNE with evidence: `CookTorranceSPF` for GGX's reason (all three lobes set `.pdf = mixPdf`, `CookTorranceSPF.cpp:205/256/316`), `GGXSPF`, `CoatedSPF`, single-lobe SPFs, and `TranslucentSPF` (whose `TranslucentBSDF::value` is a `switch` returning exactly one lobe's term per direction). **Wider consequence, deliberate**: `kray_I` is the SPF's own transport weight and is not in general `f_I cos / p_I` as the paired BSDF evaluates it (TranslucentSPF's carries Beer extinction the BSDF omits; `SchlickSPF`'s Schlick-1994 weight differs from its own BRDF integral by up to 20% at grazing, which is DL-127. A third reason this row used to give -- "the old expression's `cos` used the GEOMETRIC normal while `p_I` is a density around the SHADING normal" -- is WRONG and was struck on review 2026-09-14: `RayIntersectionGeometric::vNormal` IS the shading normal, and `Object::IntersectRay` builds `ri.onb` from it, so the old pairing used one frame, not two), so the fix moves BDPT/VCM/MLT on every material at every non-delta vertex -- always toward PT, which has used `kray` since it shipped. Residuals opened, not fixed: DL-125 (PT's and BDPT's HWSS companion fallback carries the same pattern where `ISPF::EvaluateKrayNM` declines), DL-126 (BDPT/VCM/MLT terminate at a material with no `IBSDF`), DL-127 (`SchlickSPF`'s `kray` vs its own BRDF integral) **MERGE-TARGET RE-MEASUREMENT + CONTROL (review P2-1, 2026-09-14).** On the merge target topology L reads PT 0.0601796 / BDPT 0.0632837 = **+5.16%** (final gate run 0.0601765 / 0.0632883 = +5.17%; `VCMStrategyBalanceTest`'s twin of the same topology reads PT 0.0601639 / VCM 0.0632532 = +5.14%), not the -2.00% quoted above (which was measured pre-merge). The swing is PT's, not BDPT's, and it is attributable: with `src/Library/Materials/SchlickSPF.cpp` alone reverted to its pre-Slice-0 state (isolated build, everything else identical) the same topology reads PT 0.0647011 / BDPT 0.0634197 = -1.98%, i.e. PT moved **-6.99%** and BDPT **-0.21%** when `SchlickSPF::Pdf` changed. `Pdf` enters PT only through its MIS partner arms, and DL-127 (`kray`) was not touched by Slice 0, so the swing is **DL-103's site** -- PT's un-guided escape-side partner is still the SELECTED lobe's own density while its NEE side now uses the true aggregate, so `w_bsdf + w_nee != 1` on a multi-lobe SPF. BDPT is insensitive because DL-69 made its `pdfFwd` and `pdfRev` the SAME function, so the change largely cancels in the ratio chain. A new **topology M** (`BDPTStrategyBalanceTest`) is the discriminator: identical geometry, emitter, camera and both rasterizer strings, with a `ggx_material` wall and a `lambertian_material` floor -- both immune to DL-127 and DL-103 (GGX emits ONE `ScatteredRay` per `Scatter` whose `.pdf` is already the aggregate `mixPdf`; Lambertian is single-lobe) -- and it reads PT 0.0471226 / BDPT 0.0472101 = **+0.19%**, and is unmoved by the same SchlickSPF revert (+0.18%). **Re-quoted 2026-09-17 (round-2 review P2-3/P2-4) against the TRUE merge target**, which brings DL-123 (`GGXBRDF::hemisphericalAlbedo`, which topology M's `ggx_material` wall consumes, and which the `+0.19%` figure above predates): topology M now reads PT 0.0471280 / BDPT 0.0472026 = **+0.16%** (channel-averaged over the render's 3 output channels) -- unchanged within MC noise of the pre-DL-123 figure, so DL-123 does not move this control's conclusion (M stays immune, L's residual stays Schlick-specific). So the residual on L is Schlick-specific, not a generic PT-vs-BDPT asymmetry of this geometry. The suite's standard 8% band is KEPT (2.8 pp of margin). **Per-channel branch premise**: this row's "up to 4x" figure assumes the three per-channel specular rays are three distinct lobes carrying distinct directions -- **DL-101** records that the per-channel loop REUSES one `ScatteredRay` across its lanes, so what actually reaches the container on that branch may be fewer than three; and **DL-127** records that `SchlickSPF`'s per-lobe `kray` is not that lobe's `f_I cos/p_I`, which is why row (a) of the closed-form table reads 0.802 Q at 60 deg rather than 1. **Cost (review P2-5)**: the `pdfFwd` addition makes BDPT evaluate `ISPF::Pdf` one extra time per non-delta vertex per subpath. On an all-`schlick_material` 256x256 / 256-spp BDPT render that is +19.55% user CPU over the pre-DL-69 integrator (163.22 vs 136.53 s, n=16/3); sharing one reconstructed `RayIntersectionGeometric` + `IORStack` between the vertex's two queries (`PathVertexEval::VertexPdfContext`) recovers 4.05% (156.61 s, n=8, Welch t=-6.39), leaving **+14.71%**. The residual is the second `SchlickSPF::Pdf` call itself (~690 ns since Slice 0), not the rebuild. | M | physics-bias | user-visible (BDPT/VCM/MLT renders of any multi-lobe material with overlapping-hemisphere lobes, e.g. `schlick_material`) |
| DL-98 | Found during DL-67 Slice 0 sibling audit (debt-slice0 worktree, 2026-09-14) | `IsotropicPhongSPF::Pdf`/`PdfNM` weight the diffuse-vs-specular mixture by the raw, angle-independent `MaxValue(rd)`/`MaxValue(rs)` painter albedos, the same shape of mismatch DL-67 Slice 0 fixed in `SchlickSPF` | OPEN-confirmed (static; same bug pattern, unfixed) | `IsotropicPhongSPF::Scatter`/`ScatterNM` (`IsotropicPhongSPF.cpp:141-142`) compute `specular.kray = pRs->GetColor(ri) * ((N+2.0)/(N+1.0)) * r_max(cos_o,0.0)`, `cos_o = dot(specular.ray.Dir(), n)` -- a function of the SPECULAR lobe's own drawn direction, exactly analogous to `SchlickSPF`'s fresnel-boosted specular kray. `diffuse.kray = pRd->GetColor(ri)` (`:184`) is direction-independent, also exactly analogous. `PTScatterSelectWeight`/`PTRandomlySelect` (`PathTracingIntegrator.cpp:1338-1356`) select between these two REALIZED krays the same way for every SPF. But `IsotropicPhongSPF::Pdf`/`PdfNM` (`:293-294`, `:317-318`) weight by `dWeight=MaxValue(rd)` / `sWeight=MaxValue(rs)` -- the raw painter albedo, missing the `(N+2)/(N+1)*cos_o` term entirely -- so the specular coefficient is wrong at every `wo` (not just non-normal incidence: even head-on the `(N+2)/(N+1)` scale factor alone is missing). Unlike `SchlickSPF`'s fix, no averaging approximation is even needed here for the diffuse side (it is already direction-independent and exactly `MaxValue(rd)`); only the specular coefficient needs the exact-at-`wo` correction. `tests/SPFPdfConsistencyTest.cpp`'s existing `IsotropicPhong` row documents "massive cross-val divergence (38k-45k mismatches out of 50k samples)" from this exact cause. **Recipe (REWRITTEN 2026-09-14 — the original prescription here was itself defective and is withdrawn).** The withdrawn text said to fix `sWeight` to the exact-at-`wo` value and leave `dWeight = MaxValue(rd)` as "already exact, no averaging needed", i.e. a diffuse coefficient `dW/(dW+sW(wo))` that VARIES WITH THE QUERY DIRECTION. That is wrong for the same reason DL-67 Slice 0's first attempt was: the diffuse lobe's selection probability is decided by the SPECULAR lobe's independent draw, not by the direction the diffuse lobe happened to produce, so its coefficient is a CONSTANT expectation and can never be direction-dependent. The correct construction (see [DL67_SLICE0_SCHLICK_PDF_WEIGHTS.md](DL67_SLICE0_SCHLICK_PDF_WEIGHTS.md) §1-§2) is `C_D = E_{(u,v)~U[0,1]^2}[P(the diffuse ray wins | that specular draw)]`, equivalently `1 - E_{omega_S~p_S}[q_S(omega_S) 1{accepted}]`, estimated by a DETERMINISTIC stratified quadrature that replays `IsotropicPhongSPF`'s own specular sampler (`GenerateSpecularRay`) in its own inverse-CDF parametrisation, reproducing its accept-checks and `RandomlySelect`'s `freeidx==1` short-circuit; `q_S(wo)` alone is exact at the query direction, as `sWeight = MaxValue(rs)*(N+2)/(N+1)*r_max(cos_o(wo),0)`. Note that the specular lobe's REJECTION rate is expected to dominate `C_D` here as it does in Schlick. Red-prove with the two gates `tests/SchlickSPFPdfConsistencyTest.cpp` now uses — a two-sided `|int Pdf - P(Scatter emits anything)| < 1%` hemisphere-quadrature gate, and a total-variation gate against a 600k-draw histogram of real `Scatter`+`RandomlySelect` draws with a threshold derived from the bin noise — NOT the per-lobe lower-bound check that file used to carry, which is an algebraic identity post-fix and was deleted. While there, check `IsotropicPhongSPF::Pdf`'s use of a 3-channel AVERAGE exponent (`Navg`) against `Scatter`'s per-branch `N[0]` / per-channel `N[i]`, the same branch-parity defect Slice 0 fixed in Schlick. See [DL67_SLICE0_SCHLICK_PDF_WEIGHTS.md](DL67_SLICE0_SCHLICK_PDF_WEIGHTS.md) §8. | S | physics-bias | user-visible (`phong_material` guided/BDPT/VCM renders and NEE partner MIS weight) |
| DL-99 | Found during DL-67 Slice 0 sibling audit (debt-slice0 worktree, 2026-09-14) | `AshikminShirleyAnisotropicPhongSPF::Pdf`/`PdfNM`'s lobe weights are not the realized selection weights `Scatter` produces: beyond evaluating at the mirror direction, they carry a SPURIOUS `Rs` factor, DROP the `cos_o` factor, and use a diffuse weight that is missing `(1-Rs)` and carries a spurious `1/pi` | OPEN-confirmed (static; partially-mitigated variant of the same pattern, unfixed) | **Description CORRECTED 2026-09-14** — the original row called this "an approximation" (mirror-direction evaluation of an otherwise-right formula). It is not: the weights are wrong by FACTORS, independently of which direction they are evaluated at. Verified by reading both functions. (a) `AshikminShirleyAnisotropicPhongSPF::Pdf` (symbol `Pdf`, the `wSpec` assignment) computes `wSpec = rs_val * specFactor_m`, but `Scatter` (symbol `Scatter`, the `specular.kray` assignment in the non-per-channel branch) sets `specular.kray = RISEPel(1,1,1) * specFactor * cos_o` with NO `Rs` factor -- its own comment says so explicitly ("specularFactor already includes Fresnel (which contains Rs), so no extra Rs multiplication"). So `Pdf` multiplies by `Rs` where `Scatter` does not. (b) `Pdf` also drops the `* cos_o` that `Scatter`'s kray carries. (c) `wDiff = MaxValue(Rd) * (28/(23*pi)) * fromK(cos_i)^2`, but `Scatter`'s `diffuse.kray = Rd * (1-Rs) * (28/23) * fromK1(cos_o) * fromK2(cos_i)` -- so `Pdf` is missing the `(1-Rs)` factor entirely and carries a spurious `1/pi` (the pi cancels against the cosine pdf in the kray derivation and must not reappear in a selection weight), on top of using `fromK(cos_i)^2` where the realized weight uses `fromK1(cos_o)*fromK2(cos_i)`. The mirror-direction evaluation is a separate and much smaller issue than any of these. `tests/SPFPdfConsistencyTest.cpp`'s existing `AshikminShirleyAnisotropicPhong` row still documents "large mismatch (16k-43k)". `tests/SPFPdfConsistencyTest.cpp`'s existing `AshikminShirleyAnisotropicPhong` row still documents "large mismatch (16k-43k)" despite this existing mitigation. Recipe: the same two gates DL-98's rewritten recipe names (two-sided `int Pdf` vs the measured emission probability; TVD against a real `Scatter`+`RandomlySelect` histogram), applied to `AshikminShirleyAnisotropicPhongSPF`. Fix (a)/(b)/(c) first -- they are factor errors and need no new machinery -- then reconstruct `specFactor`/`diffFactor` at the ACTUAL query `wo` and give the diffuse lobe a CONSTANT `C_D` expectation rather than a direction-dependent coefficient (see DL-98's recipe for why). NOT lower priority than DL-98, as this row previously claimed on the strength of the withdrawn "deliberate partial mitigation" reading. See [DL67_SLICE0_SCHLICK_PDF_WEIGHTS.md](DL67_SLICE0_SCHLICK_PDF_WEIGHTS.md) §8. | S-M | physics-bias | user-visible (`ashikmin_shirley_material`, lower severity than DL-98) |
| DL-100 | Found during DL-67 Slice 0 fix pass (debt-slice0, 2026-09-14) | `SchlickSPF`'s specular sampler builds its half-vector in the UNFLIPPED `ri.onb`, so on a back-face hit every specular draw is rejected by `Scatter`'s own accept-check and the material silently loses its entire specular lobe | OPEN-confirmed (static; sampler-side energy loss, deliberately NOT fixed in Slice 0) | `SchlickSPF.cpp`'s `GenerateSpecularRay` took an `onb` parameter and never used it: the half-vector was always built from `ri.onb.u()/v()/w()`, while `Scatter`/`ScatterNM` pass the FlipW'd `myonb` and then accept-check against `myonb.w()`. On a back-face hit (`dot(ri.ray.Dir(), ri.onb.w()) > NEARZERO`, i.e. single-sided geometry hit from behind -- double-sided meshes pre-flip `vNormal`, so they never reach it) the two frames differ by sign, the sampled `h` sits in the wrong hemisphere, and `dot(dir, myonb.w()) > 0` fails for every draw. Result: a back-face `schlick_material` hit scatters diffuse-only, at full diffuse weight, with no specular energy at all. Slice 0 REMOVED the dead parameter (so the frame in use is now explicit) and made `ComputeSchlickSpecularPdf` use `ri.onb` too, which makes the DENSITY correct for the sampler as it stands -- it returns 0 there, exactly matching what `Scatter` emits. The sampler itself is untouched, because fixing it changes what renders sample and needs its own red-proof. Recipe: build a single-sided geometry (e.g. `ClippedPlaneGeometry`, or a non-double-sided mesh) with a `schlick_material`, put the camera behind it, and count `ScatteredRay`s of type `eRayReflection` out of `Scatter` at a back-face hit (expect 0 pre-fix); fix by passing the caller's `myonb` into `SchlickSampleHalfVector` instead of reading `ri.onb`, then re-point `ComputeSchlickSpecularPdf` at the same frame and re-run `SchlickSPFPdfConsistencyTest` (which must stay green -- it exercises front-face hits only) plus a new back-face row. | S | energy-loss | user-visible (back-face `schlick_material` hits: specular lobe entirely absent) |
| DL-101 | Found during DL-67 Slice 0 fix pass (debt-slice0, 2026-09-14); dynamically confirmed during round-2 review (2026-09-14) | The per-channel specular loop in `SchlickSPF` and both Ward SPFs REUSES one `ScatteredRay` across its three lanes, so a lane whose sampler declines to write a direction pushes the PREVIOUS lane's direction carrying this lane's kray and pdf | OPEN-confirmed (static AND dynamic: a duplicate-direction counter on real `Scatter()` output found 111613/200000 corrupted draws on a grazing, low-then-high per-channel-roughness config; that config's gate TVD, 0.01424, drops to 0.01009 -- a real but partial reduction -- when the reuse is patched out; see `tests/SchlickSPFPdfConsistencyTest.cpp`'s `DL-101 KNOWN-FAILURE` row; unfixed) | `SchlickSPF::Scatter` declares `ScatteredRay d, s;` once and its per-channel branch calls `GenerateSpecularRay(s, ...)` three times in a loop. `GenerateSpecularRay` writes `specular.ray` ONLY when `hdotk > 0`; otherwise the ray is left untouched. On lane 0 that is harmless (a default-constructed `Ray` has direction `(0,0,0)`, so the accept-check rejects it), but on lanes 1 and 2 the untouched ray still holds the PREVIOUS lane's direction -- which generally passes the accept-check -- and the branch then assigns lane `i`'s `kray[i]` and lane `i`'s `ComputeSchlickSpecularPdf(..., rt.v[i], it.v[i])` to it. The container receives a ray at lane `j`'s direction priced as lane `i`. `hdotk <= 0` is reachable for any roughness (the theta warp reaches `pi/2` as `xi -> 0`) and becomes common at grazing incidence with high roughness. Same code shape, verified by reading: `WardIsotropicGaussianSPF::Scatter` and `WardAnisotropicEllipticalGaussianSPF::Scatter` (both conditionally `ray.Set` inside `if(hdotk>0)` and both reuse one `s` across the per-channel loop). NOT present in `IsotropicPhongSPF` (its generator sets the ray unconditionally) or `AshikminShirleyAnisotropicPhongSPF` (its generator returns `false` and the caller gates on it). Slice 0's `Pdf` quadrature models the INTENDED semantics (a lane whose `hdotk <= 0` is rejected), so closing this row removes a modelling gap as well as the sampler bug. Recipe: instrument the per-channel branch to count pushes whose direction is bit-identical to the previous lane's while `hdotk <= 0` for the current lane, on a grazing high-roughness fixture with an `RGBScalarPainter` roughness; fix by declaring the `ScatteredRay` INSIDE the loop (or having `GenerateSpecularRay` return a bool the caller gates on, as `AshikminShirleyAnisotropicPhongSPF` already does) in all three files. | S | physics-bias | user-visible (`schlick_material`/`ward_*_material` with a per-channel roughness painter, at grazing incidence) |
| DL-103 | Found during DL-67 Slice 0 round-2 review (debt-slice0, 2026-09-14) | Slice 0's fix makes `SchlickSPF`'s AGGREGATE `Pdf()`/`PdfNM()` correct, but un-guided default PT's escape-side MIS partner is still the SELECTED lobe's own per-lobe density, not that aggregate -- so `w_bsdf + w_nee != 1` at a Schlick vertex even after Slice 0, in ordinary (non-guided) PT | OPEN-confirmed (measured on a Schlick-material furnace, un-guided default PT) | `LightSampler.cpp`'s NEE arms weight against `pMaterial->Pdf(...)` (`LightSampler.cpp:2545,2708`, `PdfNM` twins `:3121,3244`) -- the AGGREGATE density Slice 0 fixed. But `PathTracingIntegrator.cpp` PART 3's escape-side partner, with guiding inactive, is `misBsdfPdf = effectiveBsdfPdf` (`PathTracingIntegrator.cpp:3836`), stored as `rs2.bsdfPdf = effectiveBsdfPdf; rs2.bsdfMisPdf = misBsdfPdf;` (`:3867-3868`), where `effectiveBsdfPdf = pS->isDelta ? 0 : pS->pdf` (`:3521`) -- the SELECTED lobe's own per-lobe density, not the material's aggregate `ISPF::Pdf()`. At a single-lobe SPF the two coincide, so this is invisible there; at a multi-lobe SPF like Schlick they are different quantities, so the NEE/escape MIS pair does not partition to one even after Slice 0's fix. This is a DIFFERENT instance than DL-67 (DL-67 is scoped to the GUIDED one-sample/RIS candidate branches, `PathTracingIntegrator.cpp` PART 3's guided block and `BDPTIntegrator.cpp`'s eye/light RIS; this row is the UN-GUIDED default-PT escape/NEE pair, which DL-67's own text does not name) and a different mechanism than DL-69 (BDPT/VCM ordinary throughput). Measured (sample-weighted `w_bsdf+w_nee` across three Schlick-material furnace configurations, un-guided default PT): 1.0102 / 1.0627 / 1.0667 at this slice's HEAD vs 1.0530 / 1.0735 / 1.0697 on pre-slice master `32824325` -- Slice 0's fix IMPROVES the partition (the aggregate it corrected is closer to the per-lobe density than the old, more wrongly-weighted aggregate was) but does not close it; worst single directional bin measured 1.353. Recipe: make the escape side's `misBsdfPdf`/`bsdfMisPdf` the material's AGGREGATE `Pdf()` (not the selected lobe's `pS->pdf`) when guiding is inactive -- the DL-74 two-roles design (`bsdfPdf` vs `bsdfMisPdf`) already separates the two fields, so this is a matter of what value populates `bsdfMisPdf` in the un-guided branch, not new machinery; the same fix should be checked against every other multi-emit SPF DL-67's own row lists (`PolishedSPF`/`PolishedBRDF`, `TranslucentSPF`, `CompositeSPF`, `BioSpecSkinSPF`, `GenericHumanTissueSPF`). Red-proof: a `PTGuidingMISPartitionTest`-style closed-form furnace on `schlick_material` with guiding OFF, gating `w_bsdf+w_nee` to 1 within MC noise (the existing `PTGuidingMISPartitionTest` only exercises GUIDED configurations, so it does not see this row). Cross-references: DL-67 (guided PT/BDPT candidates, still open), DL-69 (BDPT/VCM ordinary throughput). See [DL67_SLICE0_SCHLICK_PDF_WEIGHTS.md](DL67_SLICE0_SCHLICK_PDF_WEIGHTS.md) SS5. **Render-level evidence added (round-2 review P2-3/P2-4, 2026-09-17):** `tests/BDPTStrategyBalanceTest.cpp` topology L (`TestSchlickMultiLobe`, DL-69's own multi-lobe `schlick_material` wall+floor scene) is this row's mechanism made concrete at full-render scale, not just a furnace. The DL-69 row's own isolated one-file A/B (`src/Library/Materials/SchlickSPF.cpp` alone reverted to its pre-Slice-0 state, everything else identical, same topology re-rendered) measured PT move -6.99% (0.0647011 -> 0.0601765) against BDPT's -0.21% (0.0634197 -> 0.0632883) when `SchlickSPF::Pdf` changed. BDPT's `pdfFwd`/`pdfRev` are the SAME aggregate function since DL-69, so BDPT is close to insensitive to the Pdf rewrite; PT's un-guided escape-side partner is still the selected lobe's density (this row's defect), so PT is NOT insensitive -- a 7% render-level swing from touching only the density Slice 0 fixed. **This resizes the row from S-M to M**: it is not only a furnace-level MIS-weight defect but a measured double-digit-percent driver of topology L's PT reference in a real regression suite. **Consequence for the suite's gate**: `BDPTStrategyBalanceTest`/`VCMStrategyBalanceTest` topology L's 8% band is marked PROVISIONAL pending this row -- PT is not an integrator-free reference for a `schlick_material` scene while this row is open (there is no closed form or furnace for topology L's full multi-bounce geometry, so an integrator-free reference is not currently available; see the topology's own comment in `BDPTStrategyBalanceTest.cpp`). A pass on topology L is not evidence BDPT is correct to the quoted percentage, only that BDPT and (a possibly biased) PT agree to within that band. | M | physics-bias | user-visible (default, un-guided PT renders of `schlick_material` and any other multi-lobe, multi-emit material; NEE partner MIS weight; drives a measured 7% PT-side swing on `BDPTStrategyBalanceTest` topology L) |
| ~~DL-102~~ | ~~Found during DL-67 Slice 0 fix pass (debt-slice0, 2026-09-14)~~ CLOSED 2026-09-14 — `eb328217`, `SchlickSPFPdfConsistencyTest` `Checks: 34 Failures: 0` | ~~`ComputeSchlickSpecularPdf`'s azimuthal density for `isotropy != 1` was a DIFFERENT DISTRIBUTION from the one `GenerateSpecularRay` draws -- not a mis-scaled version of it~~ The density now matches the sampler's own warp | CLOSED-by-test | The helper returned `p/(2 pi (cos^2 phi + p^2 sin^2 phi))`, with a comment asserting it was "the phi PDF (from the sampling inversion)". The sampler draws, per quadrant, `phi = (pi/2) sqrt(p^2 v^2/(1 - v^2 + v^2 p^2))` with `v ~ U[0,1)`; inverting, `v = t/sqrt(p^2 + t^2(1-p^2))` with `t := phi/(pi/2)` measured from the quadrant's own axis, so the true density is `p^2/(2 pi (p^2 + t^2(1-p^2))^(3/2))`. Both forms integrate to 1 over `[0,2pi)` and both agree at `p=1`, which is how it survived. Against a 2 000 000-draw histogram of the real sampler the shipped form was off by up to 25x (`p=0.3`, `phi=1.702`: 0.4526 vs 0.0181 true; `p=0.6`, same `phi`: 0.2575 vs 0.0674); the corrected form matches the histogram to MC noise at `p` = 1, 0.8, 0.6 and 0.3. Gated by `SchlickSPFPdfConsistencyTest`'s anisotropic rows (isotropy 0.6 and 0.8) via both the normalisation and total-variation gates. Filed as its own row rather than folded into DL-67 because it is a different bug with a different mechanism, and a future reader looking for "why did anisotropic Schlick MIS weights change" needs to find it. | S | physics-bias | user-visible (`schlick_material` with `isotropy != 1`: NEE/BDPT/VCM MIS weights) |
| DL-125 | Found during DL-69's sibling audit (debt-dl69 slice, 2026-09-14) | PT's and BDPT's HWSS COMPANION-wavelength throughput falls back to an aggregate-BSDF-over-per-lobe-pdf pairing whenever the SPF does not implement `ISPF::EvaluateKrayNM` | OPEN-confirmed (static; the identical pattern DL-69 measured at 2.00x on the hero path) | `PathTracingIntegrator.cpp`'s HWSS body asks `pSPF->EvaluateKrayNM( ri.geometric, pS->ray.Dir(), pS->type, swl.lambda[w], iorStack )` for each companion wavelength and, when that returns -1 (the `ISPF` base-class default, i.e. "not implemented"), falls back to `compWeight = pBRDFCur->valueNM(...) * cosTheta / pS->pdf` -- the material's AGGREGATE spectral BSDF over the ONE selected lobe's conditional density, exactly DL-69's pairing. The hero wavelength is correct (`heroScatterNM = pS->krayNM * invSelectProb`), so the defect is a per-companion over-count that grows with the number of overlapping non-delta lobes. Exactly TWO classes override `EvaluateKrayNM` in the whole tree (`grep -rn 'Scalar EvaluateKrayNM' src/Library/`): `PolishedSPF` and `HairBSDF`. `CoatedSPF`, `FabricSPF` and `WeaveSPF` each carry a comment saying they deliberately do NOT override it, and their reasoning is what BOUNDS this row: `CoatedSPF.cpp`'s block explains that the fallback routes to `valueNM * cos / pS->pdf` and that `pS->pdf` is "the true hero mixture pdf that actually drew it", so the fallback is EXACT -- but only because those SPFs store a MIXTURE density on every emitted ray. The defect is therefore confined to the SPFs that store a PER-LOBE conditional density instead and do not override: `SchlickSPF`, `WardIsotropicGaussianSPF`, `WardAnisotropicEllipticalGaussianSPF`, `IsotropicPhongSPF`, `AshikminShirleyAnisotropicPhongSPF`, `CompositeSPF` -- i.e. exactly DL-69's affected set minus `PolishedSPF`, which the override already covers. `GGXSPF` and `CookTorranceSPF` are immune here for the same reason they were immune to DL-69, which is TWO things and not the "their lobes all carry `mixPdf`" this row used to give alone (review correction, 2026-09-14): `Scatter`/`ScatterNM` select the lobe INTERNALLY (`if( uLobe < pDiffuseSelect ) ... else if ... else`) and emit exactly ONE `ScatteredRay`, so `selectProb` is identically 1 AND that one ray's `.pdf` is the aggregate `mixPdf`; single-emit with a per-lobe `.pdf` would still be mispaired. `BDPTIntegrator.cpp`'s two HWSS companion loops were given the SAME ladder by the DL-69 fix (`19d97505`), deliberately mirroring PT rather than diverging from it, so both integrators now carry one shared residual instead of two different ones. Recipe: implement `EvaluateKrayNM` on `SchlickSPF` first (its per-lobe `kray` is a closed form -- `pDiffuse->GetColorNM` for the diffuse lobe, `rho + (1-rho)*fresnel` for the specular, both already computed inside `ScatterNM`), then red-prove with a `hwss TRUE` spectral render of DL-69's own topology L against the `hwss FALSE` twin; extend to the other five SPFs. **That probe is now IN TREE and MEASURED** (review P2-6, 2026-09-14): `BDPTStrategyBalanceTest`'s `TestSpectralHWSSCompanionLadder` renders topology L with `bdpt_spectral_rasterizer`, `hwss FALSE` at 1024 spp against `hwss TRUE` at 256 spp, and reads ACHROMATIC means 0.064099 vs 0.0633116, ratio **0.987717 (-1.23%)**; three independent runs read -1.24% (both sides at 256 spp), -1.23% and -1.09%, all negative and of the same magnitude across a 4x change in the hero-only sample count, so the ~-1.2% is systematic with a ~0.15 pp run-to-run spread (which is why the gate band is 5%, not something tight). Two things to carry into the fix. (a) The recipe's "per-channel means must agree" is NOT a usable gate: a hero-only render draws one wavelength per path, leaving several percent of purely chromatic MC noise on each channel of what is a grey scene (measured 0.0656/0.0626/0.0641 at 1024 spp), so the ACHROMATIC mean is the statistic and the per-channel ratios are printed but not gated. (b) The measured sign is OPPOSITE to a naive over-count and the magnitude is nowhere near the 2.00x DL-69 measured on the hero path -- so on this scene the companion fallback's error is dominated by RISE's separately documented, pre-existing HWSS spectral-bundle bias, and closing DL-125 should NOT be expected to move a render by anything like 2x. **Control added (round-2 review P3-4, 2026-09-17):** `TestSpectralHWSSCompanionLadderControl` runs the IDENTICAL ladder on topology M (`ggx_material` + `lambertian_material`) -- `GGXSPF` also does not override `EvaluateKrayNM`, so it ALSO takes the fallback on every companion wavelength, but for `GGXSPF` the fallback computes the SAME thing the DL-69-fixed per-lobe path would (single emitted lobe, `.pdf == mixPdf`, `kray` already `f_I cos/mixPdf`) -- i.e. the fallback is EXACT on this material, not merely reachable, so a residual on M cannot be DL-125.  Measured on this branch: topology L achromatic ratio 0.988877 (**-1.11%**), topology M (control) achromatic ratio 0.990212 (**-0.98%**) -- M's gap is ~88% the size of L's, so ~88% of the hwss FALSE/TRUE gap is material-independent HWSS bias (the same pre-existing spectral-bundle effect (b) above already names), and DL-125's own share is the DIFFERENCE, ~0.13 pp, inside the "≈0.1-0.3 pp" order of magnitude a first pass at this control estimated.  **Not evidence from this row**: CLAUDE.md's "hwss=true env-only 18% under" figure (PT env-MIS + volume transport arc bullet) is a VCM/env-IBL measurement on a completely different scene (uniform environment light, no surfaces) and mechanism -- it must not be cited as corroborating or explaining this row's ~1 pp companion-fallback residual, and this row's control measurement says nothing about that figure either. | M | physics-bias | user-visible (HWSS spectral renders of any multi-lobe material: PT, BDPT, VCM, MLT) |
| DL-126 | Found during DL-69's sibling audit (debt-dl69 slice, 2026-09-14) | BDPT/VCM/MLT eye and light subpaths TERMINATE at any material whose `GetBSDF()` returns null, losing every path through `biospec_skin_material` and `generic_human_tissue_material` past the first hit | OPEN-confirmed (static; the null return and the `break` are both unconditional) | `BioSpecSkinMaterial::GetBSDF()` and `GenericHumanTissueMaterial::GetBSDF()` both `return 0`. `PathVertexEval::EvalBSDFAtVertex` returns zero for a null BSDF, and `BDPTIntegrator.cpp`'s non-delta throughput branch in BOTH `GenerateEyeSubpathImpl` and `GenerateLightSubpathImpl` guards on `if( PositiveMagnitude<Tag>( f ) <= 0 ) { break; }` -- so a subpath that scatters off one of these materials dies there, in every tag (RGB, NM, HWSS) and in VCM and MLT which share those generators. PT is unaffected (it prices continuations from `pS->kray`, never from the material's `IBSDF`). Before DL-69 that guard was load-bearing (`f` WAS the throughput numerator); after `19d97505` the throughput no longer reads `f` at all on the ordinary path, so the guard is vestigial there and the fix is plausibly just narrowing it -- but removing it changes renders of these materials with no existing coverage, and it is a different bug pattern from DL-69 (energy loss by early termination, not a measure mismatch), so it was filed rather than folded in. Note the same null `GetBSDF()` also makes `vertices.back().isConnectible` false, so these vertices legitimately support no connection strategies; the loss is of the BSDF-sampled continuation, not of NEE. | DL-127 | Found during DL-69's closed-form red-proof (debt-dl69 slice, 2026-09-14) | `SchlickSPF`'s per-lobe `kray` is the Schlick-1994 sampling weight, not that lobe's `f_I cos / p_I`, so the sampled-continuation estimator and `SchlickBRDF::value`'s own hemispherical integral disagree by up to 20% | OPEN-confirmed (measured, 200000 draws per incidence vs a 200x400 BRDF quadrature) | `SchlickSPF::Scatter` sets the specular lobe's `kray = rho + (1-rho)*fresnel` while `SchlickBRDF::value` returns `(rho + (1-rho)*fresnel) * factor` with `factor = Z*A/(4*PI*nl*nv)`, and `ComputeSchlickSpecularPdf` returns `t*Z*A^2/(4*PI*hl)`; the ratio `f_S cos / p_S` over `kray_S` is therefore `hl / (nv * t * A)`, not 1. `tests/SchlickLobePairingTest.cpp` measures the consequence directly: at `rd = rs = 0.4`, roughness 0.5, isotropy 1, the per-lobe estimator `E[kray_I/q_I]` reads 1.00001 / 0.96142 / 0.80224 of the BRDF's own quadrature integral at 0 / 30 / 60 deg incidence (re-measured 2026-09-17 on the merge target, review P2-2; the row's earlier 0.99927/0.96142/0.80200 figures were from before this branch merged master), while the aggregate-over-aggregate pairing reads 1.01051 / 0.99724 / 0.99914 -- i.e. the deficit is entirely in the SPF's `kray` convention, not in the estimator (the aggregate pairing's own small departure from 1.0 is DL-69's known ~1.1% `SchlickSPF::Pdf` residual, not this row's `kray` defect -- see `SchlickLobePairingTest.cpp`'s replicated measurement). `tests/SPFBSDFConsistencyTest.cpp` already bands this material at 15% (`{ "Schlick", schlickSPF, schlickBRDF, false, 0.15 }`) and attributes the gap to "a known limitation of the Schlick approximation at grazing angles" citing 2.1% at 30 deg and 12.7% at 60 deg -- but that band was measured at roughness 0.3, and the gap grows with roughness (20% at roughness 0.5, 60 deg). The practical consequence is an NEE-vs-BSDF-sampling inconsistency: NEE evaluates `SchlickBRDF::value` while every sampled continuation carries `kray`, so the two MIS partners at a `schlick_material` vertex are pricing different BRDFs. Affects PT, and since DL-69 (`19d97505`) BDPT/VCM/MLT identically. Recipe: derive the missing `hl/(nv*t*A)` factor into `SchlickSPF::Scatter`/`ScatterNM`'s specular `kray` (and re-check the diffuse lobe, whose `kray = rd` IS exactly `f_D cos / p_D`), then re-run `SchlickLobePairingTest` -- column (a) must land on Q at every incidence -- and re-derive `SPFBSDFConsistencyTest`'s Schlick tolerance downward from 0.15. | M | physics-bias | user-visible (every `schlick_material` render, all integrators) |

| ~~DL-72~~ | ~~DL53_RAYCASTER_ENV_ESCAPE_MIS.md: optimal-MIS training gap~~ | ~~REOPENED round-2 (P2-B): the round-1 wiring itself has a moment/count pairing defect~~ | CLOSED 2026-09-14 (round 3, debt-guiding2 slice) | Volume sites (`RayCaster.cpp` RGB+NM `CastRay`/`CastRayNM`): `rs2.bsdfTimesCos = RISEPel(phasePdf,phasePdf,phasePdf)` (was `RISEPel(0,0,0)`) -- correct per `IPhaseFunction::Pdf()==Evaluate()` and no separate cosine term for a volume vertex -- paired with a new `AccumulateCount` call. BSSRDF sites (`PathTracingIntegrator.cpp`, both continuations, both tags): new `PTBssrdfSwTimesCos` helper recovers `Sw*cos = (weight/weightSpatial)*cosinePdf` algebraically from the already-computed `bssrdfWeight`/`bssrdfWeightSpatial` split (no BSSRDF-file edits), paired with a new `AccumulateCount` before Russian roulette. Red-proof, corrected 2026-09-14 (review round 2) -- the earlier wording called `OptimalMISAccumulatorTest`'s Test 12 the red-proof, which it is not: Test 12 (`TestDL72PairedTrainingFires`, 34/0 total, was 27/0) drives the accumulator's PUBLIC API by hand and hand-derives `alpha=0.5` unwired -> `alpha=1/3` buggy (unpaired) -> `alpha=4/10.4` fixed (paired), which pins the ACCUMULATOR'S ARITHMETIC but executes no line of `RayCaster.cpp` or `PathTracingIntegrator.cpp` and so is blind to whether either file calls the API at all -- exactly what both DL-72 regressions were. The real red-proof is the new `tests/OptimalMISTrainingSitesTest.cpp`, which runs BOTH production sites (an optically thin global fog under an environment; a real `SubSurfaceScatteringMaterial` on a real sphere `Object` through production `IntegrateFromHit`) and reads `Solve()`'s branch table back: with the two files reverted to `ddf05c6c` (the round-2 "leave the arm UNWIRED" state) the volume site reads exactly clampMin **0.001** and the BSSRDF site exactly clampMax **0.999**; wired, they read **0.857** and **0.929** -- 7/0. P2-5 (review round 2): the volume sites additionally recorded the moment against the RAW `phasePdf` while the direction came from the guided mixture; `bsdfPdf` is now `effectivePdf` (the true density) and the raw phase pdf moved to the new `bsdfMisPdf` field, where DL-73 requires it -- see the DL-74 row. Gate: `RayCasterEnvEscapeMISTest` 91/91, `SSSRadianceScalingTest` 574017/0, `MISWeightsTest` 59/0, `RasterizerDefaultsConsistencyTest` 164/0, all unchanged; `make -C build/make/rise -j8 all` clean. Review round 3 (same slice): the red-proof asserted only `Solve()`'s alpha, which is a RATIO and so is unmoved by a `bsdfTimesCos` scaled by any constant -- precisely this row's failure shape. `OptimalMISAccumulator` gained a const `GetTileTraining()` accessor and `OptimalMISTrainingSitesTest` now asserts the trained QUANTITY: at the volume site every escape contributes exactly `(L_env*phasePdf)^2/phasePdf^2 = L_env^2`, so the accumulated sum must be a whole multiple of `L_env^2` not exceeding the attempt count (measured **484 x L_env^2 over 726 attempts**), and at BOTH sites re-running identical seeds under a 3x brighter uniform environment must give exactly **9x** (measured 9 and 9). Red-proved by mutating both volume sites to `rs2.bsdfTimesCos = RISEPel(1,1,1)`: sum inflates to **76430.2 = 484 x (4*PI)^2**, two assertions fail (17/2), while the alpha assertion stays green. 19/0 after. **Review round 4 (same slice) corrected the trained QUANTITY itself.** `Sw*cos` is the BSSRDF exit's integrand with the AREA-measure `weightSpatial = Rd*Ft(exit)/pdfSurface` DROPPED, and `LightSampler`'s entry-NEE arm dropped the identical factor from the other side of the same pair (the caller applies it after the call returns); the accumulator's moment is of the INTEGRAND (Kondapaneni 2019), so both halves were scaled by an arbitrary, non-O(1) amount and skewed the alpha of every tile an SSS surface touches. Since `weight = weightSpatial*Sw*PI` and `cosinePdf = cos/PI`, the integrand is exactly `weight*cosinePdf` -- a multiply, not a ratio, so `PTBssrdfSwTimesCos` becomes `PTBssrdfTrainedBsdfTimesCos(weight, cosinePdf)`, its Rd==0 guard disappears and the Pel form stays per-channel instead of collapsing to `MaxValue()`. `EvaluateDirectLighting{,NM}` gain a trailing `neeTrainingScale` (default 1) that scales the TRAINING integrand only, never the returned radiance; the two BSSRDF/random-walk entry-NEE call sites pass `PTSurvivalMagnitude(bssrdfWeightSpatial)`. The alternative offered in review -- leaving the site untrained, as DL-84 leaves PT's in-loop volume site -- was rejected because it silences the BSDF half while the NEE half, governed by a per-scene accumulator pointer rather than a per-call flag, goes on training the same mis-scaled moment UNPAIRED. Red-proof (the `L_env^2` law cannot see this -- a radiance scale multiplies right and wrong numerators alike): a decorator scales the diffusion profile's `Rd` by 2 and forwards every sampling method verbatim, the walk gets a large path importance so `RayCaster`'s own `rs.importance < RC_RR_THRESHOLD` test cannot fire for either run, and the accumulator is wired into the `LightSampler` so the NEE half is measured too; identical attempt counts confirm identical paths, and every moment must then scale by exactly 4 (bounded below 4 by the tile's Rd-independent SSS-surface events). On `145c9259` BOTH ratios were EXACTLY **1**; after, **3.99962** (BSDF) and **3.99567** (NEE). `OptimalMISTrainingSitesTest` **23/0** (21/2 before). See [DL72_RAYCASTER_BSDFTIMESCOS_TRAINING.md](DL72_RAYCASTER_BSDFTIMESCOS_TRAINING.md) "Round 3" and "Round 5". | S | precision | not user-visible (training-input fix only; render-time weight was already `PowerHeuristic`-correct before and after -- see the row's own prior "not applicable" note) |
| ~~DL-74~~ | ~~DL72_RAYCASTER_BSDFTIMESCOS_TRAINING.md: surface env-NEE/escape MIS partition violation under guiding~~ | ~~`PathTracingIntegrator.cpp`'s surface scatter continuation stores the COMBINED `effectiveBsdfPdf`... but surface env-NEE weights against the RAW material pdf~~ | CLOSED 2026-09-14 (debt-guiding2 slice, review round 2) for EVERY guiding mode; **DL-83 closes with it** | **The first repair (same slice, earlier commit) did NOT close the partition and its ledger entry overstated it; this row supersedes that text.** Root cause restated: the guided pdf had two jobs and one home. `RAY_STATE::bsdfPdf` was simultaneously the throughput/optimal-MIS-training denominator (which REQUIRES the true sampling density) and the MIS partner against light sampling (which requires a function `LightSampler`'s NEE arms can evaluate for the same direction — impossible, since NEE runs at PART 2 before PART 3 selects a lobe). Fix: split the roles. `bsdfPdf` keeps the true sampling density; a new `RAY_STATE::bsdfMisPdf` (default -1 = "not set, use `bsdfPdf`", read via `MisPartnerPdf()`) carries a lobe-INDEPENDENT NOMINAL density `alpha_nom*guide(w) + (1-alpha_nom)*p_aggregate(w)` evaluated identically by the env-NEE arms, the area-light NEE arms, the escape weight and the emitter-hit weight. `alpha_nom` = base alpha with the learned per-cell scaling and NO per-lobe halving; the cosine product is applied unconditionally; the guiding MODE does not enter the formula, which is why DL-83 closes here. MIS is unbiased for any weights that partition to one (one-line proof in the doc), so a shared nominal density costs nothing in correctness. Also: ONE distribution per shading point (initialised above PART 2 with ONE `sampler.Get1D()`, shared with PART 3 — removes the extra NEE-side draw AND the second stochastic region lookup; nothing is drawn when guiding is off); a direction with NO BSDF-side partner keeps weight 1 on BOTH sides (a delta lobe; a vertex with no guiding at all) -- **but not a direction merely outside the material's aggregate SAMPLING support, which rounds 2-3 wrongly put in that class; see the round-4 amendment at the end of this row**; the volume continuations get the same split (DL-72 P2-5). Red-proof: new `tests/PTGuidingMISPartitionTest.cpp`, two closed-form furnaces on the live Job/RayCaster/LightSampler. Red on `8fcce0bf` (18/4): (b) `guidingLearnedAlpha=true`, the DEFAULT, +63.3%; (c) `eRayReflection` lobe +47.5%; (d) area-light NEE vs the guided emitter hit -42.7%; (e) RIS mode -65.9%, with the guiding-off reference at +0.08% and control row (a) at +0.16%. Green after: **22/0**, every row within 0.55% of its closed form. **Review round 3 (same slice, same day) found that the round-2 split had left two things undone and fixed both; the row stays CLOSED only because the two new red-proof rows are green.** (P1) Splitting one field into two obliges an audit of every CONSUMER of the old one, and `bsdfMisPdf` was consumed in exactly ONE place (`RayCasterEnvEscapeMISWeight`): `PathTracingShaderOp`'s three entry points still forwarded only `rs.bsdfPdf` into `IntegrateFromHit{,NM,HWSS}` (which then re-derived the partner as the same value, under a comment asserting every caller is non-guided -- false for the one producer round 2 had just taught to differ), and `EmissionShaderOp`'s RGB+NM emitter-hit weights read `rs.bsdfPdf` in the partner role. Global medium + trained VOLUME guiding + an area emitter: **+44.1% over** (row (g), guided 0.0111283 vs the unguided expectation 0.0077211) on the round-2 HEAD `2ebcaff9`, **+0.041%** after. (P2) The round-2 residual list dismissed the NEE-vs-escape IOR-stack divergence as "pre-existing and orthogonal"; both adjectives were wrong -- the escape side never evaluated the aggregate pdf at all before this row, and an albedo-1 furnace at a vertex inside a dielectric read **-14.0% with guiding OFF and -14.8% ON** (row (f)), because `PolishedSPF::Pdf` (Fresnel from `ior_stack.top()`: 0.266774 at top 1.0 vs 0.296169 at 1.5) and `TranslucentSPF::Pdf` (`containsCurrent()`: 0 vs 0.185406) really do vary with the stack. `EvaluateDirectLighting{,NM}` now take the caller's live `IORStack` (PT's PART 2 and HWSS NEE sites, and `DirectLightingShaderOp`, pass theirs; the volume and BSSRDF NEE sites keep the null default because neither of their materials reads a stack). Stated consequence: a guiding-OFF render of a stack-reading material changes -- it was mis-weighted before. `PTGuidingMISPartitionTest` **37/0** (was 22/0; 34/3 on `2ebcaff9`). **Review round 4 (same slice, same day) found no P1 and one more live partition defect at the design's own edge, fixed under this row.** Rounds 2-3 read `p_aggregate(w) <= 0` as "the BSDF-sampling technique never generates `w`" and gave weight 1 to BOTH sides; under guiding that is false, because the continuation is drawn from a MIXTURE whose guide term reaches directions the material's own sampler cannot (PT's one-sample branch traces the guided direction whenever `combinedPdf > NEARZERO`, which `alpha*guide` alone satisfies), so that whole region's energy was counted TWICE. Reachable set: any direction with a nonzero BSDF value but zero aggregate sampling density -- the four full-sphere BSDFs (hair, fabric, weave, translucent), whose NEE hemisphere rejection is deliberately disabled, and any partial-support lobe. Fix: `PTGuidingMisPdf::Eval` returns the full mixture whenever guiding is ACTIVE (passing the aggregate through untouched when it is not, so "no partner" survives where it is true), and the four NEE arms call the hook OUTSIDE their `pdf > 0` gates, gating the weight on the BLENDED value. Red-proof, two new rows on the tilted-hemisphere decorator SPF (`Pdf` EXACTLY zero outside a hemisphere 45 degrees off N, albedo-1 Lambertian BRDF over the whole upper hemisphere): (h) env-NEE vs the env escape with the guide aimed into that wedge, **+10.57 %** on `0860f78f` (+12.23 % at 4x samples, converging on the closed-form doubled-wedge excess `(1-cos45)/2 = 14.6 %`) -> **-0.35 %**; (i) area-NEE vs the emitter hit with the emitter ENTIRELY inside the wedge, **+100.35 %** -> **-0.03 %**, its closed form being `L_e*(R/d)^2*cos(beta)`. Both rows carry a guiding-OFF CONTROL, green before and after. `PTGuidingMISPartitionTest` **63/0** (61/2 on `0860f78f`). Round 4 also forwarded the MIS partner through the fourth HWSS delegation (latent -- no producer can currently reach it with a differing value), and corrected two doc overclaims: the legacy shader chain still does NOT partition (`Reflection`/`Refraction`/`DistributionTracing`/`FinalGather` ops all continue with a default-constructed `RAY_STATE`, so `MisPartnerPdf()` is 0 and `EmissionShaderOp` weights at 1), and the hoisted unconditional `ApplyCosineProduct` changes the guided SAMPLING distribution for glossy lobes as well as the weight (unbiased -- the throughput divides by the post-product `combinedPdf` -- but a variance change, unmeasured). A clay_lights concern raised in review was REFUTED by reading: PART 3's `pSPF` is `pClaySPF` under the override and `ClayNEEMaterial::Pdf` delegates to the same instance, so both sides evaluate one function. See [DL74_ENV_NEE_GUIDING_PARTITION.md](DL74_ENV_NEE_GUIDING_PARTITION.md) §8 and §9. | M | physics-bias | user-visible (guided PT with an environment OR area light, every guiding mode) |
| ~~DL-75~~ | ~~RayIntersectionGeometric.h: `bGeomNormalOrientedToRay`/`bGeomNormalRayDerived`; HairGeometry.cpp ~:1029~~ | ~~SSS on hair geometry has no defined outward entry normal~~ Characterized and narrowed: the entry continuation is built from the shading normal (`vNormal`/Ncyl, a genuine per-hit cylindrical radial-outward direction, NOT the ray-derived ribbon-plane `vGeomNormal`/Nflat DL-75 was filed about), so it can never point into the wrong hemisphere by construction; the disk-to-surface area-Jacobian input (`entryGeomNormal`=Nflat) remains ray-derived and is the accepted, documented residual | CLOSED 2026-09-14 (consistency pin; residual narrowed and documented, not eliminated) | `tests/HairSSSEntryNormalTest.cpp`: a real `HairGeometry` strand + real `SubSurfaceScatteringMaterial` driven through 500 real `BSSRDFSampling::SampleEntryPoint` trials plus a white-box sub-test pinning `HairGeometry`'s own `bGeomNormalOrientedToRay`/`bGeomNormalRayDerived` contract directly — `valid 500/500  badFinite 0  badUnitNormal 0  badContinuationHemisphere 0  badNeeAdapter 0  offStrand 0`.  This is a consistency pin, not a red-proof: the invariant it asserts (`Dot(scatteredRay.Dir(), entryNormal) >= 0`) already held structurally before this pass, because `SampleEntryPoint`'s cosine-weighted continuation is built via `cosineONB.CreateFromW(entryNormal)` — a cosine-hemisphere sample around any axis satisfies that dot-product bound by construction, regardless of what the axis physically means for a sub-micron fibre.  The production NEE adapter (`BSSRDFAdapters::BSSRDFEntryBSDF`) was also confirmed to return a finite, non-negative value on a hair entry record rather than crash or NaN.  **What remains UNDEFINED, accepted rather than fixed**: `entryGeomNormal` (Nflat, ray-derived per `bGeomNormalRayDerived`) drives `SampleEntryPoint`'s `cosN`/`cosT`/`cosB` disk-to-surface area-Jacobian terms, so the SAME physical point on a fibre can report a DIFFERENT Jacobian depending on which probe axis/direction reached it — a `subsurfacescattering_material` on `hair_geometry` therefore renders something well-defined (finite, non-inverted, non-crashing) but its BSSRDF energy/PDF is not claimed physically correct, matching the row's own original recipe option (a) ("characterize... likely inconsistent/incorrect but not crashing") rather than option (b) (a reject/diagnostic path, not implemented — the combination is not forbidden and does not corrupt the walk).
